Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Um… I’m probably here because I’ve been more stressed than I’d like to admit. Since my in-laws moved into our house, I’ve just been really keyed up and worn down.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I’ve been snapping at my wife, avoiding calls I’d normally take care of right away, and… I almost let a musician’s contract fall apart because I couldn’t think straight. That’s not really like me, so I thought I should come in before it gets any worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of fear or anxiety that aren't related to your in-laws or current situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I’m generally not a fearful person, and I haven’t noticed anxiety coming out of nowhere or about unrelated things. It feels pretty tied to what’s been going on at home, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say sad most of the time, no. I mean… I’ve been worn out and not myself, but “sad or down” doesn’t feel like the main thing. If anything, it’s more like I’m strained, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had anything like a panic attack. At least not the way I understand it. I’ve definitely felt stressed and tense, but I haven’t had those sudden overwhelming episodes, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts or urges to do something over and over?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. Not urges to repeat things or anything like that. I might go over things in my head sometimes, but I think that’s more just… normal stress? I haven’t noticed myself needing to do things over and over. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to perform specific rituals or behaviors to cope with your stress?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I have anything like rituals. I mean, I might have little ways of trying to keep myself calm, like taking a minute or stepping away, but not specific behaviors I feel I have to do. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong need to control or order things in your environment when you're feeling stressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a strong way. I mean, I like things to feel manageable, especially when the house feels tense, but I’m not, like, arranging things or needing everything in a certain order to get through it. At least I haven’t noticed that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're doing things excessively or more than you would like to in order to cope with your current situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not excessively, at least. I mean, I’m trying to cope the best I can, but I don’t feel like I’m doing anything over and over or more than I should to manage it. Maybe I’m not thinking of the right kind of thing, though—what would count as excessive?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, my sleep hasn’t been something I’ve been tracking closely. I might be a little more restless some nights, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern since they moved in. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding places, people, or activities more than usual since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit, I guess. I wouldn’t say I’m avoiding people completely, but since they moved in I’ve been putting off calls I’d normally return right away, especially work calls. I keep telling myself I’ll handle them when I’m clearer, but then I just… don’t. It’s embarrassing, honestly, because that’s not like me. Does that count as avoidance?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any feelings of being detached from yourself or your surroundings since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean… I’ve felt overwhelmed, but not like I’m outside of myself or that things around me aren’t real. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're observing yourself from outside your body or as if you're in a dream since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or events that you would normally participate in since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have, more than I’d like to admit. I’m not trying to cut people off, exactly, but since my in-laws moved in I’ve been dodging calls I’d normally take right away, especially work-related ones. I keep thinking I just need a minute to get my head straight, and then I put it off.\n\nIt’s not my usual way of handling things. I mean, I manage people for a living, so it feels… pretty bad to say. I almost let a musician’s contract fall apart because I couldn’t think clearly enough to deal with it. That’s not really like me, so I thought I should come in before it gets any worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of fear or anxiety that aren't related to your in-laws or current situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I’m generally not a fearful person, and I haven’t noticed anxiety coming out of nowhere or about unrelated things. It feels pretty tied to what’s been going on at home, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say sad most of the time, no. I mean… I’ve been worn out and not myself, but “sad or down” doesn’t feel like the main thing. If anything, it’s more like I’m strained, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had anything like a panic attack. At least not the way I understand it. I’ve definitely felt stressed and tense, but I haven’t had those sudden overwhelming episodes, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts or urges to do something over and over?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. Not urges to repeat things or anything like that. I might go over things in my head sometimes, but I think that’s more just… normal stress? I haven’t noticed myself needing to do things over and over. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to perform specific rituals or behaviors to cope with your stress?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I have anything like rituals. I mean, I might have little ways of trying to keep myself calm, like taking a minute or stepping away, but not specific behaviors I feel I have to do. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong need to control or order things in your environment when you're feeling stressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a strong way. I mean, I like things to feel manageable, especially when the house feels tense, but I’m not, like, arranging things or needing everything in a certain order to get through it. At least I haven’t noticed that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're doing things excessively or more than you would like to in order to cope with your current situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not excessively, at least. I mean, I’m trying to cope the best I can, but I don’t feel like I’m doing anything over and over or more than I should to manage it. Maybe I’m not thinking of the right kind of thing, though—what would count as excessive?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern since they moved in. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding places, people, or activities more than usual since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit, I guess. I wouldn’t say I’m avoiding people completely, but since they moved in I’ve been putting off calls I’d normally return right away, especially work calls. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to perform specific rituals or behaviors to cope with your stress?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I have anything like rituals. I mean, I might have little ways of trying to keep myself calm, like taking a minute or stepping away, but not specific behaviors I feel I have to do. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong need to control or order things in your environment when you're feeling stressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a strong way. I mean, I like things to feel manageable, especially when the house feels tense, but I’m not, like, arranging things or needing everything in a certain order to get through it. At least I haven’t noticed that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're doing things excessively or more than you would like to in order to cope with your current situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not excessively, at least. I mean, I’m trying to cope the best I can, but I don’t feel like I’m doing anything over and over or more than I should to manage it. Maybe I’m not thinking of the right kind of thing, though—what would count as excessive?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, my sleep hasn’t been something I’ve been tracking closely. I might be a little more restless some nights, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern since they moved in. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding places, people, or activities more than usual since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit, I guess. I wouldn’t say I’m avoiding people completely, but since they moved in I’ve been putting off calls I’d normally return right away, especially work calls. Does that count as avoidance?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any feelings of being detached from yourself or your surroundings since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're observing yourself from outside your body or as if you're in a dream since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Um… I’m probably here because I’ve been more stressed than I’d like to admit. Since my in-laws moved into our house, I’ve just been really keyed up and worn down.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I’ve been snapping at my wife, avoiding calls I’d normally take care of right away, and… I almost let a musician’s contract fall apart because I couldn’t think straight. That’s not really like me, so I thought I should come in before it gets any worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of fear or anxiety that aren't related to your in-laws or current situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I’m generally not a fearful person, and I haven’t noticed anxiety coming out of nowhere or about unrelated things. It feels pretty tied to what’s been going on at home, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say sad most of the time, no. I mean… I’ve been worn out and not myself, but “sad or down” doesn’t feel like the main thing. If anything, it’s more like I’m strained, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had anything like a panic attack. At least not the way I understand it. I’ve definitely felt stressed and tense, but I haven’t had those sudden overwhelming episodes, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts or urges to do something over and over?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. Not urges to repeat things or anything like that. I might go over things in my head sometimes, but I think that’s more just… normal stress? I haven’t noticed myself needing to do things over and over. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to perform specific rituals or behaviors to cope with your stress?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I have anything like rituals. I mean, I might have little ways of trying to keep myself calm, like taking a minute or stepping away, but not specific behaviors I feel I have to do. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong need to control or order things in your environment when you're feeling stressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a strong way. I mean, I like things to feel manageable, especially when the house feels tense, but I’m not, like, arranging things or needing everything in a certain order to get through it. At least I haven’t noticed that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're doing things excessively or more than you would like to in order to cope with your current situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not excessively, at least. I mean, I’m trying to cope the best I can, but I don’t feel like I’m doing anything over and over or more than I should to manage it. Maybe I’m not thinking of the right kind of thing, though—what would count as excessive?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, my sleep hasn’t been something I’ve been tracking closely. I might be a little more restless some nights, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern since they moved in. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding places, people, or activities more than usual since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit, I guess. I wouldn’t say I’m avoiding people completely, but since they moved in I’ve been putting off calls I’d normally return right away, especially work calls. I keep telling myself I’ll handle them when I’m clearer, but then I just… don’t. It’s embarrassing, honestly, because that’s not like me. Does that count as avoidance?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any feelings of being detached from yourself or your surroundings since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean… I’ve felt overwhelmed, but not like I’m outside of myself or that things around me aren’t real. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're observing yourself from outside your body or as if you're in a dream since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve been stressed and kind of not myself, maybe, but not like I’m watching myself from the outside or like things are dreamlike. I haven’t noticed anything like that. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or events that you would normally participate in since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have, more than I’d like to admit. I’m not trying to cut people off, exactly, but since my in-laws moved in I’ve been dodging calls I’d normally take right away, especially work-related ones. I keep thinking I just need a minute to get my head straight, and then I put it off.\n\nIt’s not my usual way of handling things. I mean, I manage people for a living, so it feels… pretty bad to say. I almost let a musician’s contract fall apart because I couldn’t think clearly enough to deal with it. Does that make sense as what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this level of stress and strain since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been about a month, I think. Pretty much since they moved into our home. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but that’s when the stress really started feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your ability to perform daily tasks or responsibilities since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’d like to say I’m still handling things, and mostly I am, but there have been some real slips.\n\nAt work, I nearly lost a musician’s contract because I just wasn’t on top of it the way I normally would be. And my managing partner actually questioned whether I could still be trusted with major negotiations, which was… hard to hear.\n\nAt home, it’s been bad enough that my wife pushed for us to get outside help. I don’t want to make myself sound incapable, but yes, it has started affecting responsibilities in a way that’s not normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding your in-laws or specific interactions with them within your home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose I am, though I don’t love admitting that. I wouldn’t say I’m being intentionally rude or anything, but I do try to steer clear of certain conversations with them at home because I’m already so keyed up.\n\nSince they moved in, I’ve been really worn down, and I think I’m not handling it as well as I’d like. I end up snapping at my wife more than I should, and then avoiding things—calls, conversations—that I’d normally just deal with. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationship with your wife since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… unfortunately, yes. I mean, I don’t think we’re falling apart or anything, and I don’t want to overstate it, but things between us have gotten more tense.\n\nThe conflict at home has become serious enough that my wife was the one pushing for us to get outside help. That was hard to hear, because I like to think I’m still managing. But between what’s been happening at home and even slipping at work—like nearly losing a musician’s contract and my partner questioning whether I can handle major negotiations—it’s definitely been affecting us. I think she’s worried, and probably frustrated with me too.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your current stress and strain are affecting your overall well-being and relationships in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I have to admit that it is. I keep wanting to say it’s just stress and I’m managing, but it’s gone past that a bit.\n\nAt work, I nearly lost a musician’s contract, which is not like me, and my managing partner actually questioned whether I could still be trusted with major negotiations. That was pretty embarrassing.\n\nAnd at home… the conflict has gotten serious enough that my wife pushed for us to get outside help. So, yes, I’d say it’s affecting my well-being and my relationships in a significant way. I’m saying that right, I hope.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
